Diffusive Limit of the Vlasov–Poisson–Boltzmann System for the Full Range of Cutoff Potentials
Weijun Wu ∗ , Fujun Zhou † and Yongsheng Li ‡

Abstract
. Diffusive limit of the Vlasov–Poisson–Boltzmann system with cutoff soft potentials − 3 < γ < 0 -3<\gamma<0 in the perturbative framework around global Maxwellian still remains open. By introducing a new weighted H x , v 2 H_{x,v}^{2} – W x , v 2 , ∞ W_{x,v}^{2,\infty} approach with time decay, we solve this problem for the full range of cutoff potentials − 3 < γ ≤ 1 -3<\gamma\leq 1 . The core of this approach lies in the interplay between the velocity weighted H x , v 2 H_{x,v}^{2} energy estimate with time decay and the time-velocity weighted W x , v 2 , ∞ W_{x,v}^{2,\infty} estimate with time decay for the Vlasov–Poisson–Boltzmann system, which leads to the uniform estimate with respect to the Knudsen number ε ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] \varepsilon\in(0,1] globally in time. As a result, global strong solution is constructed and incompressible Navier–Stokes–Fourier–Poisson limit is rigorously justified for both hard and soft potentials. Meanwhile, this uniform estimate with respect to ε ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] \varepsilon\in(0,1] also yields optimal L 2 L^{2} time decay rate and L ∞ L^{\infty} time decay rate for the Vlasov–Poisson–Boltzmann system and its incompressible Navier–Stokes–Fourier–Poisson limit. This newly introduced weighted H x , v 2 H_{x,v}^{2} – W x , v 2 , ∞ W_{x,v}^{2,\infty} approach with time decay is flexible and robust, as it can deal with both optimal time decay problems and hydrodynamic limit problems in a unified framework for the Boltzmann equation as well as the Vlasov–Poisson–Boltzmann system for the full range of cutoff potentials. It is also expected to shed some light on the more challenging hydrodynamic limit of the Landau equation and the Vlasov–Poisson–Landau system. Mathematics Subject Classification (2020) : 35Q20; 35Q83 Keywords : Vlasov–Poisson–Boltzmann system; hydrodynamic limit; Navier–Stokes–Fourier–Poisson system; optimal time decay rate



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Description of the Problem

In this paper, we intend to investigate hydrodynamic limit of the Vlasov–Poisson–Boltzmann (briefly VPB for short)
system in the incompressible diffusive regime

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\varepsilon\partial_{t}F^{\varepsilon}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}Q(F^{\varepsilon},F^{\varepsilon}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle-\varepsilon\Delta_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}F^{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}v-1,\quad\lim_{|x|\rightarrow\infty}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle F^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=F^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v),\end{array}\right.

(1.1)

which describes the dynamics of an electron gas in a constant ion background.
More precisely,
F^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\geq 0
represents the density of rarefied gas at time
t\geq 0
, velocity
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}

and position
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, with initial data
F^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)
.
The self-consistent electric potential
\phi^{\varepsilon}(t,x)
is coupled with the density function
F^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)
through the
Poisson equation in (
1.1
), where the constant ion background charge is scaled to
1
. The Boltzmann collision operator
Q(G_{1},G_{2})
describing
the binary elastic collision between particles takes the form

\displaystyle Q(G_{1},G_{2})(v)

\displaystyle:=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}q_{0}(\theta)\big[G_{1}(v^{\prime})G_{2}(v_{*}^{\prime})-G_{1}(v)G_{2}(v_{*})\big]{\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}\omega,

where
v^{\prime}=v-[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega
and
v_{*}^{\prime}=v_{*}+[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega
by the conservation of momentum and energy

v^{\prime}+v_{*}^{\prime}=v+v_{*},\quad|v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}=|v|^{2}+|v_{*}|^{2}.

The collision kernel
|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}q_{0}(\theta)
, depending only on

|v-v_{*}|
and
\cos\theta=\omega\cdot\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}
, satisfies
-3<\gamma\leq 1
and the Grad’s
angular cutoff assumption

0\leq q_{0}(\theta)\leq C|\cos\theta|.

The exponent
\gamma
is determined by the potential of intermolecular forces, and is classified into the
soft potential case when
-3<\gamma<0
and the hard potential case when
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
. In particular, the hard potential
case includes the hard sphere model with
\gamma=1,q_{0}(\theta)=C|\cos\theta|
and the Maxwell model with
\gamma=0
.
For soft potentials, the case
-2\leq\gamma<0
is called the moderately soft potential, while
-3<\gamma<-2
is called the
very soft potential
[
39
]
.
The positive parameter
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
is the Knudsen number, which equals to the ratio of the mean free path to the macroscopic length scale.

Global existence and long time behavior of solutions to the standard VPB system, i.e. (
1.1
)
with
\varepsilon=1
, have been paid a lot of attention. In the framework of renormalized solutions, Lions
[
35
]
constructed
global large solutions for initial value problem, and Mischler
[
37
]
established global large solutions for
initial and boundary value problem. In the perturbative framework around global Maxwellian, Guo
[
20
]

firstly constructed global existence of classical solutions in the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{3}
for moderately soft
potentials
-2<\gamma<0
and Maxwell model
\gamma=0
, and established
global existence and exponential decay of classical solutions in periodic domain
\mathbb{T}^{3}
for hard sphere model
\gamma=1
in
[
21
,
22
]
.
Duan, Yang and Zhao
[
13
,
15
]
established global existence and optimal time decay estimate of
classical solutions in the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{3}
for hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
and moderately soft potentials
-2\leq\gamma<0
, respectively.
Then the analysis was extended to the full range of cutoff soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
by
[
44
]
.
Recently, Cao, Kim and Lee
[
7
]
established global strong solution and exponential time decay rate for
diffusive boundary problem with hard sphere model
\gamma=1
, and Li and Wang studied the in-flow boundary problem with soft potentials

-3<\gamma<0
. For more related works on the standard VPB system, we refer to

[
10
,
11
,
12
,
14
,
33
,
40
,
45
,
46
,
47
]
and the references cited therein.

Hydrodynamic limit of the VPB system also received an extensive research.
In the framework of renormalized solutions, Arsénio and Saint-Raymond
[
1
]
showed that renormalized solutions
of the VPB system (
1.1
) converged to dissipative solutions of the incompressible
Navier–Stokes–Fourier–Poisson (NSFP for short) system. In the perturbative framework,
Guo, Jiang and Luo
[
19
]
justified the incompressible NSFP limit of classical solutions for hard sphere
model
\gamma=1
, Li, Yang and Zhong
[
34
]
established decay estimate and incompressible NSFP limit for
classical solutions by spectral analysis for hard sphere model
\gamma=1
.
In addition, Guo and Jang
[
26
]
studied the compressible Euler–Poisson limit for the
VPB system in hyperbolic regime by truncated Hilbert expansion
[
6
]
for hard sphere model
\gamma=1
.
For more related works on hydrodynamic limit of kinetic equations, we refer
the readers to
[
2
,
3
,
4
,
8
,
9
,
16
,
17
,
27
,
28
,
29
,
30
,
31
,
36
,
38
,
41
,
42
]

and the references cited therein.

Unfortunately, hydrodynamic limit of the VPB system (
1.1
) with soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
in the perturbative framework
is still left open. Inspired by the
L^{2}
–
L^{\infty}
approach for weak solutions

[
25
,
26
]
and the higher order energy approach for classical solutions
[
21
,
22
,
24
]
,
we introduce a new weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
–
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
approach with time decay for strong solutions to solve this problem
for both hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
and soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
.
The spirit of this approach lies in the interplay between the velocity weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
energy estimate with time decay and the
time-velocity weighted
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
estimate with time decay for the VPB system (
1.1
), which
eventually leads to the uniform estimate with respect to the Knudsen number
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
globally in time. Then global existence of
strong solutions is established and convergence to the incompressible NSFP system is rigorously justified
for the full range of collision potentials
-3<\gamma\leq 1
. Meanwhile, such uniform estimate with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
also
yields the optimal
L^{2}
time decay rate as well as the optimal
L^{\infty}
time decay rate for the VPB system (
1.1
) and its
incompressible NSFP limit for the full range of collision potentials, that is,
(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}
time decay rate
in
L^{2}
space and
(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}
time decay rate in
L^{\infty}
space.

1.2.
Notations

Before stating the main results of this paper, we need some notations.

For notation simplicity, we use
X\lesssim Y
to denote
X\leq CY
, where
C
is
a constant independent of
X
,
Y
and
\varepsilon
. We also use the notation
X\sim Y
to represent
X\lesssim Y

and
Y\lesssim X
. The notation
X\ll 1
means that
X
is a constant small enough.
Throughout the paper,
C
denotes a generic positive constant independent of
\varepsilon
.
The multi-indexs
\alpha=[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}]\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
and

\beta=[\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}]\in\mathbb{N}^{3}

will be used to record spatial and velocity derivatives, respectively.
We denote the
\alpha
-th order
space partial derivatives by
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}=\partial_{x_{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{x_{3}}^{\alpha_{3}},

and the
\beta
-th order
velocity partial derivatives by
\partial_{v}^{\beta}=\partial_{v_{1}}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}^{\beta_{2}}\partial_{v_{3}}^{\beta_{3}}.

And
\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}=\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{v}=\partial^{\alpha_{1}}_{x_{1}}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{x_{2}}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{x_{3}}\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{v_{1}}\partial^{\beta_{2}}_{v_{2}}\partial^{\beta_{3}}_{v_{3}}
stands for the mixed space-velocity derivative.
In addition, the notation
\nabla_{x}^{k}=\partial^{\alpha}_{x}
will be used when
\beta=0,|\alpha|=k
. The length of
\alpha

is denoted by
|\alpha|=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}.

We use
\|\cdot\|_{L^{q}_{x,v}}
to denote the
L_{x,v}^{q}
-norm on the variables

(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
for
1\leq q\leq\infty
. For an integer
m\geq 1
, we also
employ
\|\cdot\|_{W^{m,q}_{x,v}}
to represent the norm of the Sobolev space

W^{m,q}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. In particular, if
q=2
then
\|\cdot\|_{H^{m}_{x,v}}
stands for
the norm of
H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})=W^{m,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Similarly,

\|\cdot\|_{L^{q}_{x}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{H^{m}_{x}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{W^{m,q}_{x}}
and

\|\cdot\|_{H^{m}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
stand for
the norms of the function spaces
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
,
H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
,

W^{m,q}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
and
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
, respectively.
Let
\langle\cdot\;,\;\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}
and
\langle\cdot\;,\;\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
be
the standard inner product in
L^{2}_{x}
and
L^{2}_{x,v}
, respectively.
We also define the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
,
H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
and
H^{2}_{x,v}
space endowed with the norms

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu(v)|g(x,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v,\quad\|g\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq 1}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)},

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)},

respectively, where
\nu=\nu(v)
will be given in (
1.4
).
Moreover,
define the Fourier transform of
f
with respect to the space variable
x
as

\widehat{f}(k):={(2\pi)^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(x)e^{-ix\cdot k}{\mathrm{d}}x.

1.3.
Main Results

To investigate hydrodynamic limit and optimal time decay rate of global strong solutions
(F^{\varepsilon},\phi^{\varepsilon})
to the VPB system
(
1.1
), we write

\begin{split}&F^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)=\mu+\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v),\\
&F^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)=\mu+\varepsilon\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)\end{split}

(1.2)

with the fluctuations
f^{\varepsilon}
and
f^{\varepsilon}_{0}
, where

\mu\equiv\mu(v):=(2\pi)^{-\frac{3}{2}}{e}^{-\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}}

is the global Maxwellian.
By substituting (
1.2
) into (
1.1
),
we get the following equivalent perturbation VPB system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}Lf^{\varepsilon}=\frac{\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon})}{\sqrt{\mu}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle-\Delta_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}v,\quad\lim_{|x|\rightarrow\infty}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v).\end{array}\right.

(1.3)

Here

\Gamma(f,g):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}Q(\sqrt{\mu}f,\sqrt{\mu}g)

is the nonlinear Boltzmann collision operator,

\displaystyle Lf:=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\Big[Q(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)+Q(\sqrt{\mu}f,\mu)\Big]:=\nu f-Kf,

is the linearized Boltzmann operator
and

\begin{split}\nu\equiv\nu(v)&:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}Q_{-}(\sqrt{\mu},\mu)=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}q_{0}(\theta)\mu(v_{*})\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*},\\
Kf&:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\Big[Q_{+}(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)+Q_{+}(\sqrt{\mu}f,\mu)-Q_{-}(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)\Big],\end{split}

(1.4)

where
\nu=\nu(v)
is called the collision frequency and
K
is a compact operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})
.
The null space of
L
, which we denote by
\mathrm{N}(L)
, is a five-dimensional subspace of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})

\mathrm{N}(L)=\text{span}\Big\{\sqrt{\mu},\ v\sqrt{\mu},\ \frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\sqrt{\mu}\Big\}.

The orthogonal projection of
f
onto the null space
\mathrm{N}(L)
is denoted by

\mathbf{P}f\ =\ \Big(\rho_{f}+u_{f}\cdot v+\theta_{f}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu},

(1.5)

where
\rho_{f},u_{f},\theta_{f}
represent the hydrodynamic density, velocity and temperature
of
f
, respectively. Let
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f=f-\mathbf{P}f
stand for the projection on the
orthogonal complement of
\mathrm{N}(L)
. As usual,
\mathbf{P}f
is called the macroscopic
part of
f
, and
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f
is called the microscopic part of
f
.
It is well-known that there exists a positive constant

\sigma_{0}>0
such that

\langle Lf,f\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\geq\sigma_{0}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}.

(1.6)

We introduce a velocity weight function for both hard and soft potentials

\displaystyle w\equiv w(v):=\begin{cases}(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}&\text{for hard potentials}\;0\leq\gamma\leq 1,\\[5.69054pt]
(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}&\text{for soft potentials}\;-3<\gamma<0.\end{cases}

(1.7)

Moreover, to overcome the singularity brought by the electric field force term
in the forthcoming
W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate, we introduce the following time-velocity weight
for the full range of collision potentials

w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}\equiv w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}(v):=e^{\widetilde{\vartheta}|v|^{2}},

(1.8)

where

\widetilde{\vartheta}\equiv\widetilde{\vartheta}(t):=\vartheta\Big(1+\frac{1}{(1+t)^{\sigma}}\Big)

(1.9)

for some constants
\vartheta>0
and
\sigma>0
to be determined later.

To state our first main result, we introduce the following temporal energy functional for the equivalent VPB system
(
1.3
) with hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
:

\begin{split}\interleave f^{\varepsilon}(t)\interleave^{\mathbf{h}}:=\;&\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|wf^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}},\\
\end{split}

(1.10)

where
w
is the weight function defined in (
1.7
).
Then we present the first main result on uniform estimate and time decay rate of global strong solutions to
the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) for hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, as well as its hydrodynamic limit to the incompressible NSFP system.

Theorem 1.1

(Hard potentials)
.

Let
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
,
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,
w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}
be defined in (
1.8
),
and
\widetilde{\vartheta}
be defined in (
1.9
) with
0<\vartheta\ll 1
and
\displaystyle 0<\sigma\leq\frac{1}{4}
.
Assume that

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=0

and there is a sufficiently small constant
\widetilde{\delta}_{0}>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that

\begin{split}\;&\left\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{H^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|wf^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\left\|\left(1+|x|\right)f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\widetilde{\delta}_{0}.\end{split}

(1.11)

Then the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) admits a unique global strong solution

\left(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right)
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\Big\{\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}\interleave f^{\varepsilon}(t)\interleave^{\mathbf{h}}+\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\Big\}\\
&+\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\Big\{\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\left(w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}\right)(t)\right\|_{W_{x,v}^{1,\infty}}+\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\left\|\left(w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}\right)(t)\right\|_{W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}}\Big\}\leq C\widetilde{\delta}_{0},\end{split}

(1.12)

where
C>0
is a positive constant independent of
\varepsilon
.

Moreover, assume that
there exist functions
\rho_{0}(x),u_{0}(x),\theta_{0}(x)\in H^{2}_{x}
such that

\displaystyle f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow f_{0}\;\;\text{strongly in}\;H^{2}_{x,v}\;\text{ as }\;\varepsilon\rightarrow 0,

(1.13)

where
f_{0}(x,v)
is of the form

\displaystyle f_{0}(x,v)=\Big(\rho_{0}(x)+u_{0}(x)\cdot v+\theta_{0}(x)\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}.

Then as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, there hold

\begin{split}&f^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\Big(\rho+u\cdot v+\theta\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}\;\text{ weakly-}*\text{in }t\geq 0,\text{weakly in }H^{2}_{x,v},\\
&\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\nabla_{x}\phi\quad\text{~~~ weakly-}*\text{in }t\geq 0,\text{weakly in }H^{2}_{x},\text{ strongly in }C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),\end{split}

(1.14)

where
\left(\rho,u,\theta,\nabla_{x}\phi\right)\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\right)\cap C\left(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\right)

and satisfies the incompressible NSFP system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\lambda\Delta_{x}u+\nabla_{x}p=\rho\nabla_{x}\theta,\qquad\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Big(\dfrac{3}{2}\theta-\rho\Big)+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\Big(\dfrac{3}{2}\theta-\rho\Big)-\dfrac{5}{2}\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\Delta_{x}(\rho+\theta)=\rho,\quad-\nabla_{x}\phi=\nabla_{x}(\rho+\theta),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle u(0,x)=\mathscr{P}u_{0}(x),\quad\theta(0,x)=\theta_{0}(x),\quad\rho(0,x)=\rho_{0}(x)\end{array}\right.

(1.15)

in the sense of distributions, where
\mathscr{P}
stands for the Leray projection, and
\kappa,

\lambda
and

p
represent the heat conductivity coefficient, the viscosity coefficient and the pressure, respectively.

Then we turn to consider the more challenging soft potential case
-3<\gamma<0
.
For this, we introduce a temporal energy functional for the VPB system (
1.3
) for the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<0
:

\begin{split}\interleave f^{\varepsilon}(t)\interleave_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}:=\;&\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\left\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|{w}^{-\ell}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\end{split}

(1.16)

where the weight function
w
is defined in (
1.7
) and
\displaystyle\ell\geq\frac{1}{2}
is a constant to be determined later.
Note that the weight
w^{|\beta|-\ell}
depends on the order of velocity derivative
\partial^{\beta}_{v}
and

{w}^{-\ell}=(1+|v|^{2})^{-\frac{\ell\gamma}{2}}\geq 1
for the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<0
.
Moreover, the last term in (
1.16
) on the second order space derivative is treated particularly with an extra

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
, due to the singularity brought by
\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-2}L(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}
in weighted

H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate, cf. the proof of Lemma
2.6
in Section
2
.
Then we state our second main result on the uniform estimate and time decay rate of global strong solutions to
the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) for soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
.

Theorem 1.2

(Soft potentials)
.

Let
-3<\gamma<0
,
0<\varepsilon\leq 1,

\ell_{0}>\frac{3}{4}
,
\ell\geq\max\big\{1,-\frac{1}{\gamma}\big\}+\frac{1}{2}+\ell_{0},

w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}
be defined in (
1.8
), and
\widetilde{\vartheta}
be defined in (
1.9
) with

0<\vartheta\ll 1
and
0<\sigma\leq\frac{1}{24}
.
Assume that

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=0

and there is a sufficiently small constant
\delta_{0}>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that

\begin{split}\;&\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\left\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|w^{|\beta|-(\ell+1)}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|{w}^{-(\ell+1)}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\;&+\left\|\big(1+|x|\big)f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\ell_{0})}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{0}.\end{split}

(1.17)

Then the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) admits a unique global strong solution

(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\Big\{\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}\interleave f^{\varepsilon}(t)\interleave_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}+\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\Big\}\\
&+\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 1}\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{5}{8}|\beta|}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{5}|\beta|}\left\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon})(t)\right\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\Big\}\\
&+\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=2}\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{5}{8}|\beta|}\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{5}|\beta|}\left\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon})(t)\right\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\Big\}\leq C\delta_{0},\end{split}

(1.18)

where
C>0
is a positive constant independent of
\varepsilon
.

Furthermore, if the initial condition (
1.13
) of
f^{\varepsilon}_{0}
holds, then
the global solution
\left(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right)
of the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) converges to
the solution of the incompressible NSFP system (
1.15
)
in the sense of (
1.14
).

Remark 1.3
.

This newly introduced
H_{x,v}^{2}
–
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
approach with time decay actually provides the time decay estimate
for the solution
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
for the full range of collision potentials
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, namely,

\displaystyle\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}},

which are optimal in the sense of Duhamel’s principle. This time decay rates covers the results of
[
13
,
15
,
44
]
,
which constructed the same optimal
L^{2}
time decay rate of the standard VPB system separately for hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, moderately soft potentials
-2\leq\gamma<0
and
full soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
in higher order energy space
H^{N}_{x,v}
with
N\geq 4
.

In addition, as the hydrodynamic limit of the solution
\left(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right)

to the VPB system (
1.3
) in the sense of (
1.14
) and (
4.40
), the solution

(\rho,u,\theta,\nabla_{x}\phi)
to the incompressible NSFP system (
1.15
) actually inherits the time decay rate, that is,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|(\rho,u,\theta,\nabla_{x}\phi)(t)\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}},\quad\big\|\nabla_{x}(\rho,u,\theta,\nabla_{x}\phi)(t)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}},\end{split}

which is consistent with our latest study for the incompressible NSFP system (
1.15
) by spectral analysis and
high-low frequency decomposition
[
18
]
.

Remark 1.4
.

The weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
–
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
approach with time decay integrates
the advantages of both the
L^{2}
–
L^{\infty}
approach for weak solutions
[
25
,
26
]
and the higher order
energy approach for classical solutions
[
21
,
22
]
. In fact, this
H_{x,v}^{2}
–
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
workspace
is actually the space with the lowest regularity to solve the hydrodynamic limit problem of the VPB system (
1.3
) in the perturbative framework, and
spaces with lower regularity like
L^{2}
–
L^{\infty}
or
H^{1}_{x,v}
–
W^{1,\infty}_{x,v}
fail due to the singularity brought by the nonlinear term

\varepsilon^{-1}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})
and
(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})f^{\varepsilon}
. This is different from the truncated Hilbert expansion approach, cf.
[
18
,
26
]
.
On the other hand, compared with the higher order energy approach, our weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
–
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
approach
needs less regularity on the initial data.

This weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
–
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
approach with time decay is flexible and robust, as it can deal with optimal time decay rate
problems uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and hydrodynamic limit problems when
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, in a single unified theory for both
the Boltzmann equation and the VPB system. It is also expected to shed some light on the more challenging hydrodynamic limit
of the Landau equation and the Vlasov–Poisson–Landau system.

Remark 1.5
.

The initial conditions
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=0
and

\left\|\big(1+|x|\big)f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}<\infty
in Theorem
1.1
and Theorem

1.2
are used to remove the singularity of the Poisson kernel and recover the optimal rate index

(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}
, similarly as the standard VPB system
[
11
]
.

For the soft potential case, since the dissipation rate (
2.13
) is much weaker than the instant energy (
2.12
),
we have to put the extra velocity weight
\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\ell_{0})}

in the initial data
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\ell_{0})}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}

in (
1.17
) to establish the desired time decay estimate
for the nonhomogeneous linearized VPB system (
4.4
), see the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
Moreover, when considering the time decay rate for the nonlinear VPB system (
1.3
),
we need the the extra velocity weight
\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma}=w^{-1}
in the initial data
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|w^{|\beta|-(\ell+1)}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
and
\varepsilon\left\|{w}^{-(\ell+1)}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
in
(
1.17
) to apply the time-weighted estimate on (
4.20
) and the iterative
technique (
4.22
), see (
4.24
) for more details.

1.4.
Main Ideas and Innovations

In this subsection, we outline the main ideas and innovations proposed in this work.
Our analysis is based on the weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
–
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
approach with time decay,
which consists of the velocity weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
energy estimate with time decay and the
time-velocity weighted
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
-estimate with time decay for the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
), which
eventually leads to the uniform estimate with respect to the Knudsen number
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
globally in time.

1.4.1.
Velocity weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
energy estimate with time decay

Our velocity weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
energy estimate contains some new ingredients.
We start with the standard
L^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate for the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\underbrace{-\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v}_{:={D}_{1}}+\underbrace{\Big\langle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}}_{:={D}_{2}}+\cdots,\end{split}

where the semi-positivity of
L
in (
1.6
) has been used.
Unfortunately, the term
D_{1}
cannot be controlled by the weak dissipation due to the collision frequency

\nu\sim\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}\sim(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\;\text{ for }-3<\gamma<1.

Rather than using the time-velocity weighted higher order energy approach to provide extra dissipation
[
13
,
15
]
,
we control
D_{1}
in a creative manner by weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate, that is,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{D}_{1}\leq&\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big\||v|\langle v\rangle^{{\frac{3}{4}}}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{3}_{v}}\big\|\langle v\rangle^{-{\frac{3}{4}}}\big\|_{L^{6}_{v}}\big\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{2}_{v}}|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}|{\mathrm{d}}x\\
\leq&\;\big\||v|\langle v\rangle^{{\frac{3}{4}}}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{3}_{x,v}}\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\\
\leq&\;\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}\|w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\Big)\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(1.19)

Here we have utilized the interpolation inequality to estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\||v|\langle v\rangle^{{\frac{3}{4}}}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{3}_{x,v}}\leq\;&\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big(\varepsilon^{2}\big\||v|^{3}\langle v\rangle^{{\frac{9}{4}}}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{3\gamma}{2}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}\|w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\end{split}

for the exponential weight
w_{\vartheta}=e^{\vartheta|v|^{2}}\gtrsim|v|^{3}\langle v\rangle^{{\frac{9}{4}}}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{3\gamma}{2}}
with
0<\vartheta\ll 1
,
which holds for the full range of cutoff potentials
-3<\gamma\leq 1
.
Furthermore, decomposing
D_{2}
into

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{2}=\;&\Big\langle{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(w_{\vartheta}^{-1}w_{\vartheta}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}+\Big\langle{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(\mathbf{(I-P)}f^{\varepsilon},w_{\vartheta}^{-1}w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}}),(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\end{split}

and taking
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
norm on
w_{\vartheta}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}
and
w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}
,
we are able to treat
D_{2}
as

\begin{split}D_{2}\lesssim&\;\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}\Big(\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}L_{v}^{2}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}+\varepsilon\|w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}\Big),\end{split}

(1.20)

see the proof of Lemma
2.2
.
Here we have used the well-known result
\|w_{\vartheta}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}
from the exponential decay
factor in
\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}
. Consequently, motivated by the above uniform estimates (
1.19
)
and (
1.20
), we believe that it is hopeful to close the standard energy estimate with the help of the
weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate, rather than the weighted higher order energy approach
[
13
,
15
]
.

It should be mentioned that lower regularity like
L^{2}_{x,v}
–
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
framework or
H^{1}_{x,v}
–
W^{1,\infty}_{x,v}
framework cannot
solve the hydrodynamic limit of the VPB system (
1.3
) in the perturbative framework, due to the singularity brought by the nonlinear
collision term
\varepsilon^{-1}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})
. This is different from the nonlinear term
\varepsilon^{k-2}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})
(
k\geq 2
) in
truncated Hilbert expansion approach, cf.
[
18
,
26
]
. Precisely speaking, for the energy estimate of

\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
, we deal with the difficult term related to nonlinear collision term
\varepsilon^{-1}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})
by

\displaystyle\Big\langle{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{6}L_{v}^{\infty}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{3}L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)},

which indicates that this term will be controlled once the dissipation estimate of
\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
is added into our analysis,
see Lemma
2.2
for more details. Correspondingly, to pair with the dissipation
\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
, the energy estimate of
\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
should be taken into consideration.
To conclude, we adopt the
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy space to treat the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) as below

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\quad\quad\quad\text{for hard potentials}\;0\leq\gamma\leq 1,\\[11.38109pt]
\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\left\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\quad\text{for soft potentials}\;-3<\gamma<0,\end{array}\right.

where the
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy space for soft potential case involves the weight function
w^{|\beta|}

as done in
[
24
]
, in order to control the velocity derivatives of the streaming term
v\cdot\nabla_{x}
by weak dissipation.

Another feature of our analysis is that we design different frameworks for the weighted energy estimate between the soft potential case and hard potential case.
In fact, to deduce the time decay rate of solutions for the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<0
, the starting point is
the
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy inequality

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\left\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x,v}}^{2}\lesssim\cdots,\end{split}

where
\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)
is equivalent to
\sum\limits_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\left\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}
, cf. Lemma
2.4
and Lemma
2.5
.
The microscopic dissipation above obviously contains the extra factor
\nu(v)\sim\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}
, which is degenerate for large
velocity and causes a difficulty in the time decay estimate of
f^{\varepsilon}
. Luckily, motivated by
[
15
]
,
we should consider the velocity weighted
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate
\left\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
to
deduce the time decay rate for the VPB system (
1.3
).
In fact, by applying the weighted energy estimate on the momentum system of
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\;\partial_{t}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}L((\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon})=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon})+\cdots.\end{split}

(1.21)

We are able to establish the weighted estimate on
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
with lower order space derivatives, rather than
\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 2\end{subarray}}\left\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
with the full order space derivatives.
The reason is that when applying
\nabla^{2}_{x}
to (
1.21
) and taking the
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimate with
w^{-2}\nabla^{2}_{x}(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}
, a
new trouble arises because the third order derivative in the term

\displaystyle\Big\langle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\nabla^{2}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},w^{-2l}\nabla^{2}_{x}(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

(1.22)

exceeds our
H^{2}_{x,v}
framework. We solve this difficulty by applying
\nabla^{2}_{x}
directly on the
VPB system (
1.3
) rather than on the microscopic system (
1.21
), and then taking the
L^{2}_{x,v}
inner product with

\varepsilon w^{-2}\nabla^{2}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\Big\|{w}^{-\ell}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\Big\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\Big\langle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}L((\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}),\varepsilon w^{-2}\nabla^{2}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=\cdots.

This treatment not only avoids the appearance of the third order derivative (
1.22
), but also absorbs the singularity of
\varepsilon^{-2}
in front of the
linearized Boltzmann operator
L
. In conclusion, we eventually design the following velocity weighted
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate for the soft potential
case
-3<\gamma<0

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I-P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|{w}^{-\ell}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

On the other hand, to establish the time decay estimate for the hard potential case
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, the starting points are the full

H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate and the higher order
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)+\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\cdots,\\
&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)+\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\lesssim\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\cdots,\end{split}

see (
2.54
), (
2.56
) and (
2.59
) for details.
This means that we should analyze the time decay estimates of
\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
,
\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
and
\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

by linear decay estimate and Duhamel’s formula

\begin{split}&\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-s)^{-(\frac{3}{4}+\frac{|\alpha|}{2})}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right]\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\cdots\\
\lesssim&\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-s)^{-(\frac{3}{4}+\frac{|\alpha|}{2})}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\langle v\rangle}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\cdots\quad\text{for}~|\alpha|\leq 1,\end{split}

see Lemma
4.6
for details. Therefore, the energy functional in (
1.10
)
for the hard potential case
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
has weight only for the lower order
derivatives
\|w(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
.

1.4.2.
Time-velocity weighted
W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate with time decay

To close the weighted energy estimates in Proposition
2.3
and Proposition
2.7
,
the time-velocity weighted
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
-estimate for
f^{\varepsilon}
is carried out and some innovations are proposed.

Firstly, to tackle the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate of
w_{{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}
, we multiply
w_{\vartheta}
by the first equation in (
1.3
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\left\{\partial_{t}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\right\}(w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon})+\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\nu(v)}+\frac{1}{2}{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}+2{\vartheta}{v}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right)}(w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon})\\
=\;&w_{\vartheta}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}Kf^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\right).\end{split}

We observe that the underbraced term increases linearly in
\langle v\rangle
and
cannot conserve positivity, due to the degenerate collision frequency
\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}
for the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<0
.
To overcome this difficulty, we adopt a time-velocity weighted function
w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}(v)
in (
1.8
)
as in
[
32
]
and introduce a new unknown
h^{\varepsilon}=w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}
satisfying

\begin{split}&\Big\{\partial_{t}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\Big\}h^{\varepsilon}+\underbrace{\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\nu(v)+\frac{1}{2}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}+2{\widetilde{\vartheta}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}+\vartheta\sigma\frac{|v|^{2}}{(1+t)^{1+\sigma}}\Big)}_{:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\widetilde{\nu}(v)}}h^{\varepsilon}=\cdots.\end{split}

Here the term
\frac{1}{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}+2{\widetilde{\vartheta}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}v}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}
in

\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\widetilde{\nu}(v)}
will be controlled, provided that the electric field
\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}
has the
time decay rate not slower than
(1+t)^{-1}
, cf. Lemma
3.1
for details.
In addition, straightforward calculation yields
w_{{\vartheta}}\leq w_{\widetilde{{\vartheta}}}\leq w_{2{\vartheta}}
,
which means that once the
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
-estimate of
w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}
is controlled,
then the
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
-estimate of
w_{{\vartheta}}f^{\varepsilon}
will be obtained in the meantime.
Thus, our goal is to establish the
W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}
-estimate for the new unknown
h^{\varepsilon}
, which
relies heavily on the characteristic estimate given in
Lemma
3.4
under the
a priori
assumption: there is
\delta>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\{(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}}\right\}&\leq\delta.\end{split}

(1.23)

Secondly, when handling the
L^{{\infty}}_{x,v}
-estimate of the mixed velocity derivative
\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}

(|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2,|\beta|\geq 1)
for the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<1
, we apply
\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}
to (
3.19
) and
exploit Duhamel’s principle to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{h}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\leq\;&\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\text{exp}\Big\{-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\widetilde{\nu}(\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\text{d}\tau\Big\}\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\partial_{\beta-1}^{\alpha}{h}^{\varepsilon}(s,X(s),V(s))\right|\text{d}s}_{:=D_{3}}+\cdots.\end{split}

(1.24)

Here
D_{3}
is challenging, in that the collision frequency
\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widetilde{\nu}
does not have positive lower bound.
Our treatment is based on the observation that

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}^{-1}|\nabla_{x}\partial_{\beta-1}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}|={\varepsilon}^{-\frac{4}{5}}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{8}}|{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{5}}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{8}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{\beta-1}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}|\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widetilde{\nu}|{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{5}}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{8}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{\beta-1}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}|,

where we have used a significant result that
\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widetilde{\nu}
has a lower bound
\varepsilon^{-\frac{4}{5}}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{8}}
, see (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.1
.
Then
D_{3}
can be controlled by

\displaystyle D_{3}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\text{exp}\Big\{-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\widetilde{\nu}(\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\text{d}\tau\Big\}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widetilde{\nu}(V(s))\left|{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{5}}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{8}}\nabla_{x}\partial_{\beta-1}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(s,X(s),V(s))\right|\text{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{5}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{(1+s)^{\frac{5}{8}}\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{\beta-1}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right\}.

This means that compared with the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate of
\nabla_{x}\partial_{\beta-1}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}
, the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate
of
\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}
loses a time decay factor
{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{5}}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{8}}
.
Therefore, we have to design a new time decay rate coupling with the
W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate of
h^{\varepsilon}
as below

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|{h^{\varepsilon}}(t)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}+\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=1}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{5}|\beta|}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{5}{8}|\beta|}\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\\
\;&+\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=2}\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{5}|\beta|}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{5}{8}|\beta|}\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}.\end{split}

(1.25)

This indicates that we pair the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate of the pure space derivative
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(|\alpha|\leq 2)

with the uniform time decay factor
(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}
, whereas the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate of the mixed space-velocity
derivatives
\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}

(|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2,1\leq|\beta|\leq 2)
with the time decay factor

\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{5}|\beta|}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}+\frac{5}{8}|\beta|}
.

Here, it should be mentioned that compared with the lower order
W^{1,\infty}_{x,v}
part in (
1.25
), one more
\varepsilon

in

\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=2}\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{5}|\beta|}\left(1+t\right)^{\frac{5}{4}-\frac{5}{8}|\beta|}\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}

is designed for the second order derivative, in that we employ different
manners to handle the main contribution term

\displaystyle\begin{split}&D_{4}:=\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s-\eta\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{|v^{\prime}|\leq 2N}\int_{|v^{\prime\prime}|\leq 3N}\text{exp}\Big\{-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{{\nu}(V(\tau))}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\text{d}\tau\Big\}\nu(V(s))\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\text{exp}\Big\{-\int_{s_{1}}^{s}\frac{{\nu}(V(\tau^{\prime}))}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\text{d}\tau^{\prime}\Big\}\nu(V(s_{1}))\big|w_{\widetilde{\vartheta}}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1},X(s_{1}),v^{\prime\prime})\big|\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

In fact, employing Lemma
3.4
and the Hölder inequality, one easily has

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{4}&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s-\eta\varepsilon^{2}}\cdots\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1},y,v^{\prime\prime})\big|^{p}\Big|\mathrm{det}\big(\frac{\partial X(s_{1})}{\partial v^{\prime}}\big)\Big|^{-1}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s-\eta\varepsilon^{2}}\cdots\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1},y,v^{\prime\prime})\big|^{p}\frac{2\varepsilon^{3}}{|s-s_{1}|^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{p}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\cdots\big\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1})\big\|_{L^{p}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\mathrm{d}s_{1}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{p}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{\big\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\right\},\end{split}

which suggests converting the main term
D_{4}
to
\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup\limits_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{\big\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s)\big\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\right\}
only for
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 1
, and to
\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sup\limits_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{\big\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s)\big\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right\}
for
|\alpha|+|\beta|=2
,
rather than to
\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup\limits_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{\big\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}{f}^{\varepsilon}(s)\big\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\right\}

since the later goes beyond our
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy framework.

Thirdly, in the process of deducing the
L_{x,v}^{\infty}
-estimate of
(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}{h^{\varepsilon}}
for the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<1
, we utilize the Young inequality to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|-\frac{5}{4}(1+t)^{\frac{1}{4}}h^{\varepsilon}\right|=\;&\frac{5}{4}{(1+t)}^{-\frac{5}{8}}\left[{(1+t)}^{\frac{5}{4}}|h^{\varepsilon}|\right]^{\frac{7}{10}}|h^{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{3}{10}}\\
\leq\;&\eta{(1+t)}^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\frac{5}{4}C_{\eta}{(1+t)}^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

Thus, applying Duhamel’s principle to the system of
(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}h^{\varepsilon}
, one establish

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}|h^{\varepsilon}|\\
\leq\;&-\int_{0}^{t}\text{exp}\Big\{-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\widetilde{\nu}(\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\text{d}\tau\Big\}\Big[\frac{5}{4}{(1+t)}^{\frac{1}{4}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big](s,X(s),V(s))\text{d}s+\cdots\\
\leq\;&\eta\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{\|(1+s)^{\frac{5}{4}}{h}^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right\}+C_{\eta}\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{\|h^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right\}+\cdots,\quad\text{for }~\varepsilon\in(0,1],\end{split}

implying that when handling the weighted
L^{{\infty}}_{x,v}
-estimate of
h^{\varepsilon}
with time decay, we also need to consider the
corresponding
L^{{\infty}}_{x,v}
-estimate of
h^{\varepsilon}
without time decay in the meantime.

In addition, to obtain the
{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}
-estimate of
(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\nabla_{x}^{2}{h^{\varepsilon}}
for the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<0
, we apply
\nabla_{x}^{2}
to (
3.19
) and exploit Duhamel’s principle to deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}|\nabla_{x}^{2}{h}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\;&\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\text{exp}\Big\{-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\widetilde{\nu}(\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2}}\text{d}\tau\Big\}\left|(1+s)^{\frac{5}{4}}\nabla_{x}^{3}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}{h}^{\varepsilon}(s,X(s),V(s))\right|\text{d}s}_{:=D_{5}}+\cdots,\end{split}

(1.26)

where
D_{5}
is tricky because
\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widetilde{\nu}
has no positive lower bound and

\|\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
is only coupled with the time decay factor
(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{8}}
.
Fortunately, thanks to (
3.5
), we observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|(1+s)^{\frac{5}{4}}\nabla_{x}^{3}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}|\;&\leq{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{4}{5}}(1+s)^{-\frac{5}{8}}\varepsilon(1+s)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla_{x}^{3}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{5}}(1+s)^{\frac{5}{8}}\|\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\\
\;&\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\widetilde{\nu}(V(s))\left[\varepsilon(1+s)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla_{x}^{3}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right]\left[{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{5}}(1+s)^{\frac{5}{8}}\|\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right],\end{split}

indicating that once
\varepsilon(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{{L^{\infty}_{x}}}
is bounded, then
D_{5}
will be controlled by

\displaystyle D_{5}\lesssim{\varepsilon}^{-\frac{1}{5}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left\{(1+s)^{\frac{5}{8}}\|\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right\}.

Consequently, based on the analysis above and another
a priori
assumption: there is
\delta>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\varepsilon\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\{(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{{L^{\infty}_{x}}}\right\}+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\{\|h^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}}\right\}+\varepsilon\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\{\|h^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{{W^{1,\infty}_{x,v}}}\}\leq\delta,\end{split}

(1.27)

we are able to solve the
W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate with time decay (
1.25
) for the VPB system (
1.3
)
with soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
, cf. the proof of Proposition
3.5
.

1.4.3.
Time decay estimate of the perturbation

Now we are in the position to close the velocity weighted
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate in Proposition
2.3
and
the time-velocity weighted
W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate in Proposition
3.5
by
the time decay estimate under the
a priori
assumptions (
1.23
) and (
1.27
).
Note that the time-velocity weighted
W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate (
3.17
) involves the time decay rate

(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}
for

\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}
and
\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
and the time decay rate

(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{8}}
for
\|\nabla_{x}\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
.
In contrast to the standard VPB system
[
15
]
, one major difference lies in the singularity of
{\varepsilon}^{-1}
in
front of the nonlinear term
{\varepsilon}^{-1}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})
in (
1.3
).
Therefore, we should first deduce the weighted energy estimate for the linearized system
(
4.4
) with a nonhomogeneous microscopic source in the Fourier space

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}{E}_{\ell}(\hat{f^{\varepsilon}})+\sigma_{0}{D}_{\ell}(\hat{f^{\varepsilon}})\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\|{w}^{-\ell-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{h}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2},\end{split}

(1.28)

where the factor
\varepsilon^{2}
on the right-hand side will help us to treat the singularity brought by

{\varepsilon}^{-1}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})
. Then employing the time-frequency weighted and the time-frequency
splitting technique developed in
[
15
]
, we derive the time decay estimate for the
linearized system (
4.4
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|e^{{tB_{\varepsilon}}}f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{0}e^{{tB_{\varepsilon}}}f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{J}\|{w}^{-(\ell+\frac{1}{2})}h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{{L^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau+\cdots\quad\text{for}~J\geq\frac{3}{2}.\end{split}

(1.29)

Further, (
1.29
) and Duhamel’s formula will lead to the time decay estimates of
\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
and
\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
, cf. Lemma
4.3
for details. Thus, the weighted
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate and weighted

W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate
are closed via
the temporal weighted method, see Proposition
4.1
. In addition, employing the interpolation between Hölder space and
L^{2}_{x}
space and the Riesz potential theory

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\|\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}[\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}\lesssim\left(\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

(1.30)

(see (
4.29
)), we verify the time weighted
a priori
assumptions (
1.23
) and (
1.27
)
in terms of the time decay rate
(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}
of
\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}
,

\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
and
\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
,
cf. the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

The rest of this manuscript is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we establish the velocity weighted
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate
for the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
). Section
3
concentrates on the time-velocity weighted
W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}
-estimate
with time decay. In Section
4
, we establish the global strong solution to the VPB system (
1.3
) and justify
its limit to the incompressible NSFP system, i.e. Theorem
1.2
and Theorem
1.1
.
Particularly, the rest of this manuscript will
focus on the more challenging soft potential case, while the proof for the hard potential case
will only be written for the different treatments from the soft potential case.

2.
Weighted
H^{2}_{x,v}
Energy Estimate

The following two sections concern the uniform-in-time
a priori
estimates on the solutions

(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
to the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) for the full range of cutoff potentials.
For this purpose, we suppose that the Cauchy problem (
1.3
)
admits a smooth solution
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
defined on the time interval
0\leq t\leq T
for some
0<T\leq\infty
.

The main purpose of this section is to derive the weighted
H^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate for the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
).
To this end, we begin with some basic properties of the linearized collision operator
L
and the nonlinear collision operator
\Gamma
.
We first list the velocity weighted estimates on the linearized collision operator
L
with respect to the velocity weight

w
defined in (
1.7
) whose proofs can be found in
[
13
,
43
]
.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{R}
. For any multi-index
\beta
with
|\beta|\geq 0
,
we have the following estimates about the linearized operator
L
.

(1)
If
-3<\gamma<0
, then for any small
\eta\geq 0
, there exists
C_{\eta}>0
such that

\begin{split}\left\langle\partial_{v}^{\beta}(Lf),w^{2|\beta|-2\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\right\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\geq\;&\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}-\eta\sum_{|\beta^{\prime}|\leq|\beta|}\|w^{|\beta^{\prime}|-\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta^{\prime}}f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}\\
\;&-C_{\eta}\|f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}.\end{split}

(2.1)

(2)

If
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
,
then for any small
\eta\geq 0
, there exists
C_{\eta}>0
such that

\begin{split}\left\langle\partial_{v}^{\beta}(Lf),{w}^{2\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\right\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\geq\;&\|{w}^{\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}-\eta\sum_{|\beta^{\prime}|\leq|\beta|}\|{w}^{\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta^{\prime}}f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}-C_{\eta}\|f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}.\end{split}

(2.2)

We now turn to deduce the velocity weighted estimates on the nonlinear collision term
\Gamma(f,g)
, which will be frequently
used in the uniform energy estimate. Before stating our result, we recall

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{\beta}\Gamma(f,g)=\sum_{|\beta_{0}|+|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=|\beta|}C_{\beta}^{\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta_{0}}(\partial_{v}^{\beta_{1}}f,\partial_{v}^{\beta_{2}}g),

where
\Gamma_{v}^{\beta_{0}}
is given by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{v}^{\beta_{0}}(f,g)\equiv\;

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}q_{0}(\theta)\partial_{v_{*}}^{\beta_{0}}\big[\sqrt{\mu}(v_{*})\big]\Big[f(v^{\prime})g(v_{*}^{\prime})-f(v)g(v_{*})\Big]{\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}\omega.

Then the following main result involves the weighted estimates on
\Gamma
with the velocity weight
w
defined in (
1.7
).

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{R}
. For any multi-index
\beta
with
|\beta|\geq 0
, we have the following bounds on the nonlinear term
\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(f,g)
.

(1)
For the soft potential case
-3<\gamma<0
, there holds

\begin{split}&\;\left\langle\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(f,g)+\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(g,f),w^{2(|\beta|-\ell)}\partial_{v}^{\beta}h\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\\
\lesssim&\;\left(\|w_{\vartheta}f\|_{L_{v}^{\infty}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}+\|\mathbf{P}g\|_{L_{v}^{2}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}f\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}\right)\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}h\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}.\end{split}

(2.3)

(2)
For the hard potential case
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, there holds

\begin{split}&\;\left\langle\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(f,g)+\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(g,f),{w}^{2\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}h\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\\
\lesssim&\;\|w_{\vartheta}f\|_{L_{v}^{\infty}}\|{w}^{\ell}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}\|{w}^{\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}h\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}+\|\mathbf{P}g\|_{L_{v}^{2}}\|{w}^{\ell}f\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}\|{w}^{\ell}\partial_{v}^{\beta}h\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}.\end{split}

(2.4)

Here, the velocity weight function
w_{{\vartheta}}
is given by

\displaystyle w_{{\vartheta}}\equiv w_{{\vartheta}}(v):=e^{{\vartheta}|v|^{2}}\quad\text{with}\;0<\vartheta\ll 1

(2.5)

throughout this section.

Proof.

We only prove (
2.3
), since the argument can be also applied to (
2.4
) under minor modification.
To this end, we claim that for any multi-index
|\beta|\geq 0,
there holds

\begin{split}&\;\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(f,g)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}+\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(g,f)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\\
\lesssim&\;\|w_{\vartheta}f\|_{L_{v}^{\infty}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}+\|\mathbf{P}g\|_{L_{v}^{2}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}f\|_{L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}.\end{split}

(2.6)

Once this estimate is established, we obtain (
2.3
) by the Hölder inequality. Thus, we focus on verifying (
2.6
) in the rest of the proof.

In fact, using the decomposition
g=\mathbf{P}g+(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g
, we have

\begin{split}&\;\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(f,g)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}+\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(g,f)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\underbrace{\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(f,\mathbf{P}g)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}}_{N_{1}}+\underbrace{\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(\mathbf{P}g,f)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}}_{N_{2}}+\underbrace{\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}(f,(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}}_{N_{3}}\\
\;&+\underbrace{\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}\Gamma_{v}^{\beta}((\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g,f)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}}_{N_{4}}.\end{split}

(2.7)

Note that

\partial_{v_{*}}^{\beta}\big(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})\big)\leq C\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*}),\;\;\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v)\leq Cw^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v^{\prime})w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v^{\prime}_{*}).

And using the Hölder inequality,
N_{1}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{1}\lesssim\;&\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu(v)^{-1}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v)\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})f(v^{\prime})\mathbf{P}g(v_{*}^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}\right|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\;&+\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu^{-1}(v)w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v)\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})f(v)\mathbf{P}g(v_{*}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}\right|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\Big[\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})|f(v^{\prime})|^{2}|\mathbf{P}g(v_{*}^{\prime})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\;&+\Big[\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})|f(v)|^{2}|\mathbf{P}g(v_{*})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\Big[\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v^{\prime})|^{2}|\mathbf{P}g(v_{*}^{\prime})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v^{\prime})w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v^{\prime}_{*}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\;&+\Big[\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})|f(v)|^{2}|\mathbf{P}g(v_{*})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(2.8)

Further, by the change of variables
(v,v_{*})\rightarrow(v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime})
with the unit Jacobain for the right-hand first term of (
2.8
), we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{1}\lesssim\;&\|w_{\vartheta}\mathbf{P}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v_{*})e^{-2\vartheta|v_{*}|^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}v_{*}\Big)|f(v)|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v){\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|\mathbf{P}g\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}f\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}.\end{split}

(2.9)

for some constant
0<\vartheta\ll 1
.
Similarly, we deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{2}\lesssim\;&\Big[\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|\mathbf{P}g(v)|^{2}|f(v_{*})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v)w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v_{*}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|w_{\vartheta}\mathbf{P}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}e^{-2\vartheta|v|^{2}}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v){\mathrm{d}}v\Big)|f(v_{*})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v_{*}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|\mathbf{P}g\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}f\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}.\end{split}

To handle
N_{3}
, applying the same method as the estimate of (
2.9
) on
N_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{3}\lesssim\;&\Big[\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v)|^{2}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g(v_{*})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v)w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v_{*}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|w_{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}e^{-2\vartheta|v|^{2}}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v){\mathrm{d}}v\Big)w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v_{*})|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g(v_{*})|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}v_{*}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|w_{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\end{split}

for some constant
0<\vartheta\ll 1
.
Furthermore, deducing similarly as
N_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{4}\lesssim\;&\Big[\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g(v)|^{2}|f(v_{*})|^{2}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v)w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v_{*}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|w_{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}e^{-2\vartheta|v_{*}|^{2}}w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v_{*}){\mathrm{d}}v_{*}\Big)w^{2|{\beta}|-2\ell}(v)|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g(v)|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}v\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|w_{\vartheta}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\|w^{|{\beta}|-\ell}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})g\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}.\end{split}

In summary, putting the above estimates of
N_{1}\sim N_{4}
into (
2.7
), we obtain the
desired claim (
2.6
). This completes the proof.
∎

2.1.
Weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
Energy Estimate for Soft Potentials

The main goal of this subsection is to establish the uniform
H_{x,v}^{2}
energy estimate for the VPB system (
1.3
) with soft potentials.
To be precise, we begin with decompose the perturbation
f^{\varepsilon}
into

f^{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}+(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},

(2.10)

where the macro part
\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}=\Big(a^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\cdot v+c^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}.

(2.11)

Then, we introduce the weighted instant energy functional
\mathbf{E}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{E}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)\sim\;&\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\|w^{|\beta|}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
&+\varepsilon\|{w}^{-\ell}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\end{split}

(2.12)

and the corresponding energy dissipation rate

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{D}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t):=\;&\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{x}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\;&+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|w^{-\ell}\nabla_{x}^{2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)},\end{split}

(2.13)

where
\ell>0
and the velocity weight
w
is given in (
1.7
).

We are now in a position to state the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
-3<\gamma<0,\,\ell>0
. Suppose that
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
is a solution to the
VPB system (
1.3
) defined on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}

and satisfies the
a priori
assumption

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)\leq\delta\quad\text{for}\;\;0\leq t\leq T

(2.14)

for some sufficiently small
\delta>0
, then there is
\mathbf{E}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)
satisfying (
2.12
) such that

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathbf{E}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)+\mathbf{D}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)\lesssim\left(\varepsilon^{3}\|w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon\|w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x,v}}\right)\mathbf{D}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)\quad\text{for all}\;\;0\leq t\leq T.

(2.15)

The main idea we adopt to prove Proposition
2.3
parallels to the
Boltzmann equation in
[
24
]
, that is, by combining direct energy methods
on the perturbation VPB system (
1.3
) together with
the so-called micro-macro decomposition method to acquire the missing macro dissipations.
However, the analysis for the VPB system for the whole range of soft potentials is quite complex
since we meet the challenge of overcoming the high velocity part of the external force term
v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}
and trying to deduce the energy estimate on the lower regularity space
H^{2}_{x,v}
, thus we prove it in details as follows.

To make the presentation clearly, we define
the instant energy
\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)
and the dissipation
\mathscr{D}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)

for the
H_{x,v}^{2}
energy estimate

\begin{split}\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{s}}(t):=\;&\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}},\\
\mathscr{D}^{\mathbf{s}}(t):=\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\|w^{|\beta|}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{x}},\end{split}

(2.16)

and the
instant energy
\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{s}}_{\ell}(t)
and the dissipation
\mathscr{D}^{\mathbf{s}}_{\ell}(t)

for the weighted
H_{x,v}^{2}
energy estimate

\begin{split}\mathscr{E}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t):=\;&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon\|w^{-\ell}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\\
\mathscr{D}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t):=\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2\\
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\|w^{|\beta|-\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|w^{-\ell}\nabla_{x}^{2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\;&+\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla^{2}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{x}},\end{split}

(2.17)

where
\ell>0
and the velocity weight
w
is given in (
1.7
).

First of all, we have
the following macro dissipation estimate for the VPB system (
1.3
) with soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Assume that
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
is a solution to the
VPB system (
1.3
) defined on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Then there is a temporal functional
\mathscr{E}_{int}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H_{x,v}^{1}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H_{x}^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathscr{E}_{int}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)\\
\lesssim\;&\big[\mathscr{E}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)+\varepsilon\|w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\mathscr{D}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}\end{split}

(2.18)

holds for any
0\leq t\leq T
,
where
\mathscr{E}_{int}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)
given in (
2.22
) satisfies

\displaystyle|\mathscr{E}_{int}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)|\lesssim\|a^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x,v}^{1}}^{2}.

(2.19)

Proof.

We prove this lemma by an analysis of the macroscopic equations and the local conservation laws. Firstly,
plugging (
2.10
) and (
2.11
) into the first equation in (
1.3
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}+b^{\varepsilon}\cdot v+c^{\varepsilon}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}+b^{\varepsilon}\cdot v+c^{\varepsilon}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\\
=\;&-\partial_{t}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}Lf^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon})}{\sqrt{\mu}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}).\end{split}

Fixing
t
and
x
and comparing the coefficients on both sides in front of

\big[\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}v_{j}\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}|v|^{2}\sqrt{\mu}\big]

we thereby obtain the so-called macroscopic equations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}-\frac{3}{2}c^{\varepsilon}\Big)=\mathscr{Y}_{1},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\partial_{t}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{i}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}-\frac{3}{2}c^{\varepsilon}\Big)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{i}\phi^{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{i},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{i}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{Y}_{3}^{i},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}v_{j}\sqrt{\mu}(i\neq j):&\qquad\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{i}b_{j}^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{j}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}\right)=\mathscr{Y}_{4}^{i,j},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}|v|^{2}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{Y}_{5}^{i}.\end{array}\right.

(2.20)

Here the right-hand terms
\mathscr{Y}_{1},\mathscr{Y}_{2}^{i},\mathscr{Y}_{3}^{i},\mathscr{Y}_{4}^{i,j}
and
\mathscr{Y}_{5}^{i}(i,j=1,2,3)
are all of the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\Big\langle\partial_{t}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\zeta\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\Big\langle-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}Lf^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon})}{\sqrt{\mu}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\zeta\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\end{split}

where
\zeta
is a (different) linear combination of the basis
\left[\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}v_{j}\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}|v|^{2}\sqrt{\mu}\right]
.

The second set of equations we consider are the local conservation laws satisfied
by
(a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon})
. To derive them, multiplying the first equation in (
1.3
) by the collision
invariant
(1,v,\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2})\sqrt{\mu}
and integrating the resulting identity over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we derive by
using (
2.11
) that
a^{\varepsilon}
,
b^{\varepsilon}
and
c^{\varepsilon}
obey the local macroscopic balance laws of mass, moment and energy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}a^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot b^{\varepsilon},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}b^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}+\mathscr{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot b^{\varepsilon}+\mathscr{X}_{c^{\varepsilon}}.\end{array}\right.

(2.21)

Here
\mathscr{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}
and
\mathscr{X}_{c^{\varepsilon}}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}
