\;&+\|a^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{{x}}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+(1+t)^{-\frac{15}{4}}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+(1+t)^{-\frac{15}{4}}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t)\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}J_{2}\lesssim\;&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq|\alpha|\end{subarray}}\|a^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}+\varepsilon^{2}\|w_{\vartheta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\Big)\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}\\
\;&+\Big(\|a^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\Big)\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+(1+t)^{-\frac{15}{4}}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+(1+t)^{-4}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t).\end{split}

For the terms
J_{3}
and
J_{4}
,
applying the same procedure as the estimate on

\mathscr{X}_{4}
in (
2.34
), and further using the
a priori
assumption (
1.27
), the Young inequality and (
4.1
), we deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}J_{3}\lesssim&\;\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|w_{\vartheta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\mathbf{P}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim&\;\frac{\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}\\
\lesssim&\;\frac{\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}+\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+(1+t)^{-5}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t)\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}J_{4}\lesssim&\;\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|w_{\vartheta}\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}\\
&\;+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{6}L_{v}^{2}}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{3}L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim&\;\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}+(1+t)^{-5}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t).\end{split}

Therefore, we obtain from
substituting the estimates on
J_{1}\sim J_{4}
into (
4.27
), choosing
\eta
and
\delta
small enough, and taking summation over
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left(\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2}\frac{\sigma_{0}}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\frac{15}{4}}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+(1+t)^{-\frac{15}{4}}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t).\end{split}

On the other hand, we multiply the above inequality by
(1+t)^{\frac{5}{2}+p}
and integrate
the resulting inequality with respect to
t
over
[0,t]
to yield

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&(1+t)^{\frac{5}{2}+p}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}+(1+t)^{\frac{5}{2}+p}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H_{x}^{1}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}+p}\left(\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H_{x}^{1}}^{2}\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
\;&+\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{5}{4}+p}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{5}{4}+p}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t)\\
\lesssim\;&\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}+p}\mathbf{D}^{\mathbf{s}}_{\ell}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t),\end{split}

which further implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}+(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H_{x}^{1}}^{2}\right\}\\
\lesssim\;&\kappa_{0}^{2}+(1+t)^{-p}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}+p}\mathbf{D}^{\mathbf{s}}_{\ell}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\frac{3}{2}}(t)+\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{2}(t).\end{split}

(4.28)

Step 3. Optimal time decay estimate on
\|h^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}}
.

In fact, we deduce from the interpolation inequality between Hölder space and
L^{2}_{x}
space, the Riesz potential theory and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}&\lesssim(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}[\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}]_{{C^{\frac{1}{2}}}}\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}\\
&\lesssim(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\left(\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim C_{\eta}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\eta\varepsilon^{2}(1+t)^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}.\end{split}

(4.29)

Here we have used a fact derived from the Schauder estimate and the Sobolev embedding inequality

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}[\nabla^{3}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}]_{{C^{\frac{1}{2}}}}&\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\|\nabla^{4}_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\|\nabla^{2}_{x}a^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}.\end{split}

Then we deduce from (
3.17
) and (
4.29
) that

\begin{split}\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 1}\varepsilon^{1+\frac{2}{5}|\beta|}(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{5}{4}|\beta|}\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}^{2}\Big\}\\
\;&+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=2}\varepsilon^{3+\frac{2}{5}|\beta|}(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{5}{4}|\beta|}\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}^{2}\Big\}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon\|h^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H_{x}^{1}L_{v}^{2}}^{2}\right\}+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H_{x}^{1}}^{2}\right\}\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{5}}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{E}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(\tau)\right\}.\end{split}

(4.30)

In summary, by taking a suitable linear combination (
4.26
)
\times M_{1}+
(
4.28
)
\times M_{2}+
(
4.30
)
for some suitably large positive constants
M_{1}\gg M_{2}>0
, we conclude (
4.2
). This completes the proof Proposition
4.1
.
∎

4.2.
Proof of the Main Theorem
1.2

With Proposition
4.1
in hand, we are in the position to complete the proof of Theorem

1.2
and verify the
a priori
assumptions (
1.23
), (
1.27
) and (
2.14
).

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

The proof of Theorem
1.2
consists of two steps. The global existence and time decay estimate of the VPB system (
1.3
)
uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
for soft potentials is presented in
Step 1
, and the justification
of the incompressible NSFP limit from the VPB system (
1.3
) is verified in
Step 2
.

Step 1. Global existence and time decay estimate.

First of all, the local solvability of the VPB system (
1.3
) for soft potentials can be obtained in terms of the energy functional

\interleave f^{\varepsilon}(t)\interleave_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}
given by (
1.16
) and the argument used in
[
23
]
.
The details are omitted for simplicity.

Next, assume that such a local solution

(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
has been extended to the time step
t=T
for some
T>0
, that is,
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
is a
solution to the VPB system (
1.3
) defined on

[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then we derive from the
estimate (
4.2
) that

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\ell}(t)\leq C\mathbf{\kappa}_{0}^{2},

(4.31)

for some constant
C
independent of
\varepsilon
. Therefore, the global strong solution
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
to the VPB system (
1.3
) for soft potentials can be obtained with the help of (
4.31
) and a standard continuation argument as in
[
21
]
. The details are omitted here for brevity.

Finally, the
a priori

assumptions (
1.23
), (
1.27
) and (
2.14
) follow by combining (
4.31
), (
2.38
), (
4.29
) and (
4.1
).

Step 2. Limits to the incompressible NSFP system.

The approach is similar to that one in
[
31
]
, but here we provide a full proof for completeness.

Step 2.1. Limits from the global energy estimate.

Based on (
1.12
) in Theorem
1.1
, the VPB system (
1.3
)
admits a global solution
f^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}_{x,v})
and
\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}_{x})
and the uniform global energy estimate (
4.20
),
there exists a positive constant
C
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\{\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}\right\}\lesssim C,\\
&\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}){f}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x,v}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\varepsilon,\\
&\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}){f}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.\end{split}

(4.32)

From the energy bound in (
4.32
), there exist
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}_{x,v})
and
\nabla_{x}\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}_{x})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}f^{\varepsilon}&\to f\quad\qquad\;\text{~~weakly-}*~{\text{for}~t\geq 0},\;\text{weakly in~}H^{2}_{x,v},\\
\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}&\to\nabla_{x}\phi\qquad\text{~~weakly-}*~\text{for}~t\geq 0,\;\text{weakly in}~H^{2}_{x},\end{split}

(4.33)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We still employ the original notation of sequence to denote its subsequence for convenience,
although the limit may hold for some subsequence.
From the dissipation bound in (
4.32
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\,\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}_{x,v})}\text{~~as~~}\varepsilon\to 0.\end{split}

(4.34)

Then, the convergence in (
4.33
) and (
4.34
) lead to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f=0.\end{split}

This implies that there exist functions
\rho,u,\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}_{x})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}f(t,x,v)=\Big(\rho(t,x)+v\cdot u(t,x)+\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\theta(t,x)\Big)\sqrt{\mu}.\end{split}

(4.35)

Next, we introduce the following fluid variables

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho^{\varepsilon}:=\langle f^{\varepsilon},\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\;\;\;\;u^{\varepsilon}:=\langle f^{\varepsilon},v\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\;\;\;\;\theta^{\varepsilon}:=\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\Big)\sqrt{\mu}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}.\end{split}

(4.36)

Multiplying (
1.3
) by the collision
invariant
(1,v,\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{3})\sqrt{\mu}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we derive that
\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
u^{\varepsilon}
and
\theta^{\varepsilon}
obey the local conservation laws

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle\hat{A}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=-\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle\hat{B}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=-\frac{2}{3}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon},\end{array}\right.

(4.37)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&A(v):=v\otimes v-\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3},~\quad B(v):=v\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\frac{5}{2}\Big),\\
&\hat{A}(v):=L^{-1}A(v),~\qquad\qquad\;\hat{B}(v):=L^{-1}B(v).~\end{split}

From (
1.3
) and the definition of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
in (
4.36
), we also find the equation of
\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle-\Delta_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}.

(4.38)

Furthermore,
via the definitions of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
u^{\varepsilon}
and
\theta^{\varepsilon}
in (
4.36
) and the uniform energy bound in (
4.32
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\Big\{\|\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|u^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\Big\}\leq C.

(4.39)

From (
4.33
) and the limit function
f(t,x,v)
given in (
4.35
), we thereby deduce the following convergences

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho^{\varepsilon}=\langle f^{\varepsilon},\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to\langle f,\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\rho,~\\
&u^{\varepsilon}=\langle f^{\varepsilon},v\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to\langle f,v\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=u,~\\
&\theta^{\varepsilon}=\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\Big)\sqrt{\mu}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to\Big\langle f,\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\Big)\sqrt{\mu}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\theta,\end{split}

(4.40)

weakly
-
*

for

t\geq 0
,
weakly in

H^{2}_{x}
and strongly in

H^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\to 0.

Step 2.2. Convergences to the limiting system.

Next, we deduce the incompressible NSFP system (
1.15
)
from the local conservation laws (
4.37
) and the convergences (
4.33
), (
4.34
)
and (
4.40
) obtained in the
Step 2.1
.

Step 2.2.1. Incompressibility and Poisson equation.
From the equation (
4.37
) and the energy uniform bound (
4.39
), it is easy to deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon}\to 0\end{split}

in the sense of distributions as
\varepsilon\to 0
. By using the first convergence in (
4.40
), we have

\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}
converges in the sense of distributions to
\nabla_{x}\cdot u
as
\varepsilon
tends to zero.
By uniqueness of distribution limit, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0.

(4.41)

The
u^{\varepsilon}
-equation of (
4.37
) yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle\widehat{{A}}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}-\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}.

The bound (
4.39
) implies
\varepsilon\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}\to 0
in the sense of distribution as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
With the aid of the self-adjointness of
L
, we derive from the Hölder inequality and the uniform dissipation bound
in (
4.32
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle\widehat{{A}}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t=\;&\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle{{A}}(v)\sqrt{\mu},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t\\
\lesssim\;&\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon.\end{split}

Thus,

\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle\hat{A}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to 0\;\;\text{strongly in}\;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0.

Moreover, the bounds (
4.32
) and (
4.39
) reveal that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\{\|\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\right\}\lesssim\varepsilon\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\{\|\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{2}_{x}}\right\}\leq C\varepsilon,\end{split}

which means that

\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\text{strongly in}\;L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+},H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0.

In summary, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\;\;\text{in the sense of distributions as }\;\varepsilon\rightarrow 0.

By using the convergences (
4.33
) and (
4.40
), we have

\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\nabla_{x}(\rho+\theta)+\nabla_{x}\phi\;\;\text{in the sense of distributions as }\;\varepsilon\rightarrow 0.

By uniqueness of distribution limit, we get

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho+\theta)+\nabla_{x}\phi=0.

(4.42)

Then, applying
\nabla_{x}\cdot
to (
4.42
) and combining the resulting equation with (
4.38
), by the uniform energy bound (
4.32
) and (
4.39
), we obtain the Poisson equation

\displaystyle\Delta_{x}(\rho+\theta)=-\Delta_{x}\phi=\rho.

(4.43)

Step 2.2.2. Equations of
u^{\varepsilon}
and
\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}
.
Following the standard formal derivations of fluid dynamic
limits of the Boltzmann equation (see
[
3
]
for instance), we obtain

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\hat{A}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon}-\frac{|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3}-\lambda\Sigma(u^{\varepsilon})-R_{\varepsilon,A},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\hat{B}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}=\frac{5}{2}u^{\varepsilon}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{5}{2}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon}-R_{\varepsilon,B},\end{array}\right.

(4.44)

where

\displaystyle\Sigma(u^{\varepsilon}):=\;

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon}+({\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon}})^{T}-\frac{2}{3}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3},

\displaystyle\lambda:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{10}\left\langle{A}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\hat{A}(v)\sqrt{\mu}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}},

\displaystyle\kappa:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{2}{15}\left\langle{B}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\hat{B}(v)\sqrt{\mu}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}},

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon,\Xi}:=\;

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\varepsilon\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}+\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\Big\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\varepsilon\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\frac{\nabla_{v}\left({\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}}\right)}{{\sqrt{\mu}}}\Big\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}-\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\Gamma((\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\Gamma(\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon})\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}},\quad\quad\text{with}\;\Xi=A~\text{or}~B.

For the vector field
u^{\varepsilon}
, we decompose

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon},

(4.45)

where
\mathscr{P}:=I-\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}\nabla_{x}\cdot
is the Leray projection operator and
\mathscr{P}^{\perp}={\mathrm{I}}-\mathscr{P}
.
Plugging the first equation of (
4.44
) into (
4.37
) and utilizing (
4.45
), we have

\partial_{t}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathscr{P}\text{div}_{x}(\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon})-\lambda\Delta_{x}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}=-\mathscr{P}(\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})+R_{\varepsilon,u},

(4.46)

where
R_{\varepsilon,u}
is given by

R_{\varepsilon,u}:=\mathscr{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot R_{\varepsilon,A}-\mathscr{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}).

For the equation of
\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}
, we substitute the second equation in (
4.44
) into
(
4.37
) to obtain

\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)+\frac{5}{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\theta^{\varepsilon})-\frac{5}{2}\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon}=-u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}-\frac{5}{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}\theta^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\cdot(R_{\varepsilon,B}).

(4.47)

Step 2.2.3. Convergences of
\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}
and
\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}
.
On one hand, applying
\mathscr{P}
on the
u^{\varepsilon}
-equation in (
4.37
) and Hölder inequality, we have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}=&\;\Big\|-\mathscr{P}(\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathscr{P}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}A(v)\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\mathrm{d}v\Big)\Big\|_{H_{x}^{1}}\\
\lesssim&\;\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}\|\nabla_{x}\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{2}L_{v}^{2}(\nu)},\end{split}

which immediately infers from the uniform bound (
4.32
) that

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{x}^{1})}\lesssim&\;\sqrt{T}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{x}^{1})}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{x}^{2})}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{x}^{2}L_{v}^{2}(\nu))}\\
\lesssim&\;C\sqrt{T}+C\end{split}

(4.48)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.
On the other hand, we directly derive from (
4.39
) that

\|\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{x}^{2})}\leq C

(4.49)

for all
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.
Noticing that

H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}){\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow}H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\overset{\text{continuous}}{\hookrightarrow}H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}),

(4.50)

where the embedding of
H^{2}_{x}
in
H^{1}_{x}
is compact locally and the embedding of
H_{loc}^{1}
in
H_{loc}^{1}
is naturally continuous.
Then, employing Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem
[
5
]
, the uniform bounds (
4.48
), (
4.49
) and
the embedding inequality (
4.50
), we deduce that there is a

\tilde{u}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
such that

\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\tilde{u}\;\;\text{strongly in}\;C(0,T;H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\;\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0\;\;\text{for any }\;T>0.

Further, using (
4.40
) and (
4.41
), we know that

\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}u=u\;\;\text{in the sense of distributions}\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0.

As a result, we obtain

\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow u\;\;\text{strongly in}\;C(0,T;H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\;\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0\;\;\text{for any }\;T>0,

(4.51)

where
u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
.
Similarly, we are able to deduce

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\;\;\text{weakly-}*{\text{for}\;t\geq 0},\;\text{weakly in}\;H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}),\;\text{strongly in}\;H^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0.

(4.52)

For the convergence of
\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
we deduce from (
4.37
) and Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big\|\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big\|_{H_{x}^{1}}=&\;\Big\|-u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}B(v)\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}\mathrm{d}v\Big\|_{H_{x}^{1}}\\
\lesssim&\;\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}\|\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{1}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{x}^{2}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)},\end{split}

which implies from the uniform bound (
4.32
) that

\begin{split}\Big\|\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{x}^{1})}\lesssim&\;\sqrt{T}\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{x}^{1})}\|u^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{x}^{2})}+\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}[\mathbf{D}_{\ell}^{\mathbf{s}}(t)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq&\;C\sqrt{T}+C\end{split}

(4.53)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.
On the other hand, we derive directly from (
4.39
) that

\Big\|\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))}\lesssim C

(4.54)

for all
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.
Thus, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem
[
5
]
,
the bounds (
4.53
), (
4.54
) and the embedding (
4.50
),
we deduce that there is

\tilde{\theta}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
,
such that

\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\tilde{\theta}\;\;\text{strongly in}\;C(0,T;H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\;\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0\;\text{for any }\;T>0.

Combining with the convergence (
4.40
), we deduce that

\tilde{\theta}=\frac{3}{2}\theta-\rho,
which implies

\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\frac{3}{2}\theta-\rho\;\;\text{strongly in}\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\;\;\text{as}\;\varepsilon\to 0,

(4.55)

where
\frac{3}{2}\theta-\rho\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
.

Step 2.2.4. Limit equation from (
4.46
) and (
4.47
).
For any
T>0
, choose a vector-valued test function
\psi(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
with
\nabla_{x}\cdot\psi=0
,
\psi(0,x)=\psi_{0}(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
\psi(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
with some
T^{\prime}<T
.
Multiplying
(
4.46
) by
\psi(t,x)
and integrating by parts over
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\psi{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=&\;-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}(0,x)\cdot\psi(0,x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=&\;-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},v\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}\cdot\psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.\end{split}

From the initial condition (
1.13
) in Theorem
1.1
and the convergence (
4.51
), we
deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},v\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}\cdot\psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x\rightarrow

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}\langle f_{0},v\sqrt{\mu}\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}\cdot\psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}u_{0}\cdot\psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\rightarrow\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.
As a consequence, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\rightarrow-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathscr{P}u_{0}\cdot\psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(4.56)

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.
Further, with the aid of (
4.51
), (
4.52
) and (
4.55
), we show the following convergences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathscr{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)\quad\text{strongly in}\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};L_{loc}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),\\[5.69054pt]
\lambda\Delta_{x}\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\lambda\Delta_{x}u\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\quad\text{in the sense of distributions},\\[5.69054pt]
\mathscr{P}(\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(\rho\nabla_{x}\phi)\quad\quad\quad\quad\;\;\,\quad\quad\;\text{strongly in}\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),\\[5.69054pt]
R_{\varepsilon,u}\rightarrow 0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\text{in the sense of distributions},\end{array}\right.

(4.57)

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.
Therefore, collecting (
4.56
) and (
4.57
), and using (
4.42
), we find that
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{t}u+\mathscr{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)-\lambda\Delta_{x}u&=\mathscr{P}(\rho\nabla_{x}\theta)\end{split}

(4.58)

with the initial data
u(0,x)=\mathscr{P}u_{0}(x)
.

Next, we derive the limit equation for
(\frac{3}{2}\theta-\rho)
from (
4.47
).
Indeed, for any
T>0
, choose a test function
\xi(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))

with
\xi(0,x)=\xi_{0}(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\xi(0,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}

with some
T^{\prime}<T
. Then from the initial condition (
1.13
) in Theorem
1.1
and the convergence (
4.55
), we
find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\xi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},\;\;\Big[\frac{3}{2}\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\Big)-1\Big]\sqrt{\mu}\Big\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}\xi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\partial_{t}\xi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
\to&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0})\xi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{3}{2}\theta-\rho\Big)\partial_{t}\xi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\end{split}

(4.59)

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.
Further, with the help of (
4.51
), (
4.52
) and (
4.55
), we
establish the following convergences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathscr{P}u^{\varepsilon}\theta^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow\frac{5}{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\theta)\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\,\,\text{strongly in}\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};L_{loc}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\frac{5}{2}\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta\qquad\qquad\;\qquad\quad\quad\quad\quad\;\,\,\,\text{in the sense of distributions},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow u\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi\quad\quad\quad\quad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\;\;\text{strongly in}\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathscr{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}\theta^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\cdot(R_{\varepsilon,B})\rightarrow 0\qquad\quad\quad\text{in the sense of distributions},\end{array}\right.

(4.60)

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.
Therefore, collecting the limits (
4.59
), (
4.60
) and using (
4.41
) and (
4.42
), we find
that
(\frac{3}{2}\theta-\rho)
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{t}\Big(\dfrac{3}{2}\theta-\rho\Big)+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\Big(\dfrac{3}{2}\theta-\rho\Big)-\dfrac{5}{2}\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0\end{split}

(4.61)

with the initial data

\theta(0,x)=\theta_{0}(x)
and
\rho(0,x)=\rho_{0}(x)
.

In summary, combining the limit equations (
4.41
), (
4.43
), (
4.58
) and (
4.61
), we conclude that

\displaystyle(\rho,u,\theta,\nabla_{x}\phi)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{1}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))

and its forms a solution to the incompressible NSFP system (
1.15
).
The proof of Theorem
1.2
is completed.
∎

4.3.
Time Decay Estimate of Energy for Hard Potentials

To close the energy estimate under the
a priori
assumptions (
1.23
), (
1.27
) and (
2.51
), we turn to deduce the time decay estimate of
\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)
. For this,
denote

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{\Lambda}(t):=\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(\tau)\Big\}+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(\tau)\Big\}\\
\;&+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{\varepsilon^{3}(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{W_{x,v}^{2,\infty}}^{2}\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.62)

Then we have the result about the VPB system (
1.3
) for hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
0\leq\gamma\leq 1.

Assume that

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=0

and the
a priori
assumptions (
1.23
), (
1.27
) and (
2.51
) hold true
for some small
\delta>0
.
Then, any strong solution
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
to the VPB system (
1.3
) defined on
0\leq t\leq T
with
0<T\leq\infty
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}(t)\leq C{\mathbf{\widetilde{\kappa}}}_{0}^{2}+C\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}(t)\;\;\;\text{on}\;\;0\leq t\leq T,

(4.63)

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
T
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{0}
is defined by

\begin{split}{\widetilde{{\kappa}}}_{0}:=\;&\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{H^{2}_{x,v}}+\|wf^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\big\|\big(1+|x|\big)f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}.\end{split}

To prove Proposition
4.4
, we should first find the optimal
temporal decay estimate for the linearized system (
4.4
).

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
0\leq\gamma\leq 1.

Assume

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=0

and

\||x|a_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<\infty.

Then the solution to the linearized VPB system (
4.4
) satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}e^{{tB^{\varepsilon}}}f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{0}e^{{tB^{\varepsilon}}}f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\sigma_{|\alpha|}}\Big(\||x|a_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\Big)\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-2(\frac{3}{4}+\frac{|\alpha|}{2})}\Big(\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\Big){\mathrm{d}}\tau\end{split}

(4.64)

for any
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

First of all, as in the soft potential case, it is easy to verify that
there is a time frequency functional
{E}(\hat{f^{\varepsilon}})
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}{E}(\hat{f^{\varepsilon}})\thicksim\;&\|\hat{f^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\frac{|\hat{a}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{|k|^{2}}\end{split}

for any
t>0
and
k\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and there also holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}{E}(\hat{f^{\varepsilon}})+\frac{\sigma_{0}|k|^{2}}{1+|k|^{2}}{E}(\hat{f^{\varepsilon}})\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\|{\nu}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{h}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.\end{split}

Then adopting the same method as in
[
13
]
by the
high-low frequency decomposition, we derive (
4.64
)
under the condition
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\sqrt{\mu}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=0
. The details are omitted here for brevity.
∎

Secondly, we need an additional lemma concerning the time decay estimates on the macroscopic quantity
\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
and
\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}
in terms of the initial data and
\mathbf{\Lambda}(t)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.4
, there hold

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left({\mathbf{\widetilde{\kappa}}}_{0}^{2}+\left[\mathbf{\Lambda}(t)\right]^{2}\right)

(4.65)

and

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}}\left({\mathbf{\widetilde{\kappa}}}_{0}^{2}+\left[\mathbf{\Lambda}(t)\right]^{2}\right)

(4.66)

for any
0\leq t\leq T.

Proof.

Applying Lemma
4.5
to (
4.16
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\||x|a_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\right)\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
\;&+\left[\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\left(\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right){\mathrm{d}}\tau\right]^{2}.\end{split}

(4.67)

On one hand, we derive from the property of
\Gamma
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\varepsilon\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\;&\left\|\|\nu f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+\left\|\|\nu f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|w(v)f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|w(v)f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{\Lambda}(t).\end{split}\mathscr{}

(4.68)

On the other hand, from the expression (
4.17
) of
h_{2}^{\varepsilon}
and exploiting the Hölder inequality and the definition (
4.62
) of
\mathbf{\Lambda}(t)
, we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left(\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|wf^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right)+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left(\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|wf^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right)\\
\lesssim\;&\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)+\left[\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\big[\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{\Lambda}(t).\end{split}

(4.69)

Therefore, substituting (
4.68
) and (
4.69
) into (
4.67
)
and using the basic inequalities

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{4}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}},\quad\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1+\tau)^{-3}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}},

we derive (
4.65
) immediately.

Similar to that of deducing (
4.67
) from
Lemma
4.5
, we derive from (
4.16
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}}\left(\||x|a_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\right)\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{5}{2}}\Big(\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}h_{1}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{x}h_{1}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\Big){\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
\;&+\Big[\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{5}{4}}\Big(\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}h_{2}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}\tau\Big]^{2}.\end{split}

(4.70)

Adopting a similar technique as the estimate of (
4.68
) and (
4.69
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\varepsilon^{2}\|h_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}h_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\|w(v)f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|w(v)\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\|w(v)f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{4}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{4}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-3}\left[\mathbf{\Lambda}(t)\right]^{2},\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\|h_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}h_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left(\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|w(v)f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right)+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left(\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|w(v)f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right)\\
\;&+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left(\|\nabla_{x}\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|w(v)\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right)\\
\lesssim\;&(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{\Lambda}(t).\end{split}

Thus, inserting the above two estimates into (
4.70
) and using the basic inequalities

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}},\quad\int_{0}^{t}(1+t-\tau)^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1+\tau)^{-3}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}},

we conclude (
4.66
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
4.6
.
∎

Finally, with the aid of Lemma
4.6
, we give the proof of Proposition
4.4
.

Proof of Proposition
4.4
.

Firstly, we deduce the optimal time decay estimate on
\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)
.
In fact, from (
2.52
) in Proposition
2.7
and the
a priori
assumption (
1.27
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)+\mathbf{D}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\lesssim 0.

(4.71)

Comparing the expressions of
\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)
in (
2.50
) and (
2.48
), we find that

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)+\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\gtrsim\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t).

(4.72)

Then using the inequalities (
4.72
) and (
4.71
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)+\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

Further, applying the Grönwall inequality to the above inequality and utilizing (
4.65
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(\widetilde{{\mathbf{\kappa}}}_{0}+\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}(t)\right).

This means

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{E}^{\mathbf{h}}(\tau)\right\}\lesssim{\widetilde{\mathbf{\kappa}}_{0}}+\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}(t).

(4.73)

Secondly, we turn to estimate the second term in (
4.62
).
To this end,
comparing the expressions of
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)
and
\mathbf{D}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)
in (
2.49
) and (
2.50
), we find that

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\gtrsim\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t).

Then employing this inequality, (
1.27
) and (
2.53
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)+\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(t)\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

Moreover, applying the Grönwall inequality to the inequality above and utilizing (
4.66
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(\tau)\right\}\lesssim{\mathbf{\widetilde{\kappa}}}_{0}+\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}(t).

(4.74)

Finally, we derive from Proposition
3.6
and (
4.29
) that

\begin{split}\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{\varepsilon(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{W_{x,v}^{1,\infty}}^{2}\Big\}+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|=2}\varepsilon^{3}(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L_{x,v}^{\infty}}^{2}\Big\}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon\|h^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{W^{2,\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{2}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{h}}(\tau)\Big\}.\end{split}

(4.75)

In summary, taking a suitable linear combination of (
4.73
), (
4.74
)
and (
4.75
), we prove (
4.63
). This completes the proof
of Proposition
4.4
.
∎

4.4.
Proof of the Main Theorem
1.1

With
Proposition
4.4
in hand, we are ready to give the proof of Theorem
1.1
and verify the
a priori
assumptions (
1.23
), (
1.27
) and (
2.51
).

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

The proof of Theorem
1.1
follows from a similar fasion as the proof of Theorem
1.2
, but the hard potential case here needs some different treatments.

In fact,
assume that
\eta
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{0}>0
are chosen sufficiently small constants,
we derive from the
estimate (
4.63
) that

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Lambda}(t)\leq C\mathbf{\widetilde{\kappa}}_{0}^{2}.

(4.76)

Then, the local solvability and the global existence of strong solution
(f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})
to the VPB system (
1.3
) for hard potentials can be obtained with the help of the energy functional

\interleave f^{\varepsilon}(t)\interleave^{\mathbf{h}}
given by (
1.10
), (
4.76
) and the argument used in
[
21
]
,
and the details are omitted for simplicity.
Moreover, combining (
2.38
), (
4.62
) and (
4.76
), we find that
the
a priori

assumptions (
1.23
), (
1.27
) and (
2.51
) can be closed.

Next, we derive the incompressible NSFP limit from VPB system (
1.3
) for hard potentials.
To this end, based on a uniform global energy estimate (
4.71
),
there exists a positive constant
C
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\{\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}\right\}\leq C

(4.77)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}){f}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x,v}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}){f}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim C\varepsilon^{2}.

(4.78)

The uniform energy bound (
4.77
) and the dissipation bound (
4.78
)
allow us to obtain the incompressible NSFP limit
using exactly the same methods as the proof of Theorem
1.2
. The details are omitted here
for brevity. The proof of Theorem
1.1
is thus completed.
∎
